Bolton was executive director of the Committee on Resolutions in the [Republican National Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_National_Committee) from 1983 to 1984.[^cite_note-209] Between 1997 and 2000, he worked as an assistant to [James Baker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Baker) when he was the UN [Secretary-General](162415.md) envoy to the [Western Sahara](33220.md).[^cite_note-210] Since 2006, he has been a paid Fox News contributor and a senior fellow at the [American Enterprise Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Enterprise_Institute).[^cite_note-McL-51][^cite_note-wadhams-52] For 2017, he reported an income of \$569,000 from Fox News.[^cite_note-DF180612-211] Bolton was a contributor to *[The Weekly Standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Weekly_Standard)*, an American [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_the_United_States) opinion magazine, from 1997 to 2000, and again from 2014 to 2016.[^cite_note-212]



Bolton speaks at [Chatham House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chatham_House) on foreign policy challenges facing the [Obama Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obama_Administration).

From 2013 until March 2018, Bolton was chairman of the far-right anti-Muslim [Gatestone Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatestone_Institute), which is prominent for disseminating false anti-immigrant and anti-Muslim information.[^cite_note-Przybyla-213] In 2018, the White House reported that Bolton's total income for 2017 had been \$2.2 million which included \$569,000 from Fox News and \$747,000 in speaking fees from, among others, the [Victor Pinchuk Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Pinchuk#Philanthropy) (a Ukrainian NGO), [Deutsche Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Bank), and [HSBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HSBC).[^cite_note-DF180612-211]

### 2012 presidential election

Bolton considered running for president in the [2012 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_United_States_presidential_election). He received attention in conservative circles, including the cover of the December 31, 2010, issue of *[National Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Review)* magazine. He told *[Politico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politico_\(newspaper\))*: "As I survey the situation, I think the Republican field is wide open. I don't think the party's anywhere close to a decision. And stranger things have happened. For example, inexperienced senators from Illinois have gotten presidential nominations."[^cite_note-214] In September 2011, Bolton said he would not run for president in 2012.[^cite_note-215]

During the Republican primary, Republican presidential-hopeful [Newt Gingrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newt_Gingrich) said he would ask Bolton to serve as his [Secretary of State](32293.md).[^cite_note-216] In January 2012, Bolton endorsed [Mitt Romney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitt_Romney) for the 2012 Republican nomination.[^cite_note-217]

### American Enterprise Institute

Bolton was senior vice president of the [American Enterprise Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Enterprise_Institute), a conservative [think tank](37101.md),[^cite_note-aei-218] from 1997 to 2001. At the time, he frequently wrote columns criticizing the Clinton administration's foreign policy. Bolton said Clinton's policy on Iraq was "worse than incompetent", his policy on North Korea was "egregiously wrong", and his Libya policy was a "catastrophic loss of U.S. credibility."[^cite_note-\:13-65]

After leaving the [George W. Bush Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_W._Bush_Administration) in 2006, Bolton returned to the American Enterprise Institute as a Senior Fellow. From 2010 until 2018, Bolton served as Director, Foreign and Defense Policy Studies for AEI.[^cite_note-aei-218] In Bolton's time at the American Enterprise Institute, he spoke against the policy of rewarding North Korea for ending its [nuclear weapons](21785.md) program. He said the policy would encourage others to violate nuclear non-proliferation rules so they could then be rewarded for following the rules they'd already agreed to.[^cite_note-fireecon-219]

In July 2013, Bolton was identified as a key member of [Groundswell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groundswell_group), a secretive coalition of right-wing activists and journalists attempting to advance political change behind the scenes through lobbying of high-level contacts.[^cite_note-220]

### John Bolton Super PAC

In 2013, Bolton set up the John Bolton [Super PAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_action_committee#Super_PACs). It raised \$11.3 million for Republican candidates in the 2014 and 2016 elections and spent \$5.6 million, paying [Cambridge Analytica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambridge_Analytica) at least \$650,000 for voter data analysis and digital video ad targeting in support of the campaigns of Senators [Thom Tillis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thom_Tillis) (R-N.C.), [Tom Cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Cotton) (R-Ark.), and [Richard Burr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Burr) (R-N.C.), and of former U.S. Senator (R-MA) [Scott Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Brown_\(politician\))'s unsuccessful 2014 bid for a U.S. Senate seat for New Hampshire.[^cite_note-millions-221][^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] In September 2016, Bolton announced that his Super PAC would spend \$1 million on (R-N.C.) Senator [Richard Burr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Burr)'s reelection effort by targeting ads at "social media users and Dish Network and Direct TV subscribers".[^cite_note-224]

The [Center for Public Integrity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Public_Integrity) analysed the John Bolton Super PAC's campaign finance filings and found that they had paid Cambridge Analytica more than \$1.1 million since 2014 for "research" and "survey research".[^cite_note-cpi-225] According to Federal Election Commission filings, Cambridge Analytica was paid more than \$811,000 by them in the 2016 presidential election;[^cite_note-tvb_gk-226] in the same election cycle, the Super PAC spent around \$2.5 million in support of Republican U.S. Senate candidates.[^cite_note-cpi-225]

Bolton said he aims to raise and spend \$25 million for up to 90 Republican candidates in the 2018 midterm elections.[^cite_note-cycle-227] In January 2018, Bolton announced a \$1 million advertising campaign in support of Kevin Nicholson's bid for the Republican nomination to run against incumbent Democratic Senator [Tammy Baldwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tammy_Baldwin) of Wisconsin.[^cite_note-cycle-227][^cite_note-228] The Super PAC ran an ad campaign in the Green Bay area in January 2018; on March 19, 2018, the Super PAC announced a two-week \$278,000 television and radio ad campaign in the Milwaukee area.[^cite_note-229]

Major donors to the John Bolton Super PAC are [Robert Mercer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Mercer_\(businessman\)), who gave \$4 million from 2012 to 2016; [Home Depot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_Depot) co-founder [Bernard Marcus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Marcus), and Los Angeles real estate developer [Geoffrey Palmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Palmer_\(real_estate_developer\)).[^cite_note-millions-221]

After Bolton was appointed National Security Advisor in March 2018, the John Bolton Super PAC and the John Bolton [PAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_action_committee) announced that their political activities were suspended temporarily, effective March 31, 2018. The Super PAC's FEC filings showed a balance of \$2.6 million in unspent donations at the end of March 2018.[^cite_note-nw180409-230][^cite_note-231]

### 2024 presidential election

Bolton had openly considered the idea of running for president in the [2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md) in order to secure [the Republican nomination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Republican_Party_presidential_primaries) against former President Donald Trump.[^cite_note-2024-2-232] However, he had been widely ridiculed for proposing running for president, due to bipartisan opposition to him and his policies.[^cite_note-2024-3-233][^cite_note-2024-4-234]

## 2025 indictment

Main article: [Prosecution of John Bolton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prosecution_of_John_Bolton)

### Background

On June 16, 2020, the Trump Justice Department attempted to block publication of Bolton's memoir, *[The Room Where It Happened](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Room_Where_It_Happened)*, seeking to confiscate Bolton's \$2 million advance for breach of contract, asserting he had not completed the prepublication security review as he had agreed to receive his security clearance. Bolton had submitted the book for security review in December 2019, and after months of discussions, was told on April 27 by Ellen Knight—the National Security Council's senior director for prepublication review—that no other classification issues remained. However, the White House did not provide Bolton written notice that he could proceed with publication, and in May another NSC official was asked to further review the manuscript.[^cite_note-235] On June 17, the Justice Department asked a federal judge to issue an [injunction](39343.md) to block publication of the book, which had already been printed, bound and shipped to distribution warehouses for its official release the following week. By that day, media outlets had acquired copies of the book and begun publishing articles about its contents.

In its brief filed with the court, the Justice Department provided six examples of what it asserted were classified items that remained in the book. Trump had previously asserted that any conversation with him is "highly classified." During a hearing on June 19, federal judge [Royce Lamberth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royce_Lamberth) castigated Bolton for proceeding to publish his book without formal clearance, but ruled that publication of the book could proceed.[^cite_note-236]

Bolton's attorney asserted that the White House was slow-walking the review process to prevent the book, which contained extensive harsh criticism of Trump, from being released during the 2020 election campaign. According to reporting, Trump claimed that the book contained "highly classified" information but also characterized the book as "pure fiction."[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239]

On June 21, a pirated copy of the book appeared online.[^cite_note-theap-240] The book was released on June 23.[^cite_note-\:15-241] Later that summer, the Justice Department opened a criminal investigation into whether the book revealed classified information, empaneling a grand jury that subpoenaed the publisher's communications records.[^cite_note-242]

The New York Times reported in 2021 that Justice Department had dropped its criminal inquiry of Bolton in June of that year and moved to end efforts to confiscate proceeds from his book.[^cite_note-243] However, the Times later reported in 2025 that the DOJ's criminal investigation into Bolton had actually "gained momentum in the Biden administration."[^cite_note-nytimes.com-244]

### FBI raid

FBI agents raided Bolton's home on August 22, 2025, as part of a documents investigation.[^cite_note-245] The *[New York Post](102227.md)* was the first outlet to report on this incident. The raid happened at approximately 7 a.m. when law enforcement officers entered Bolton's house, authorized by a court order, and started looking for "classified materials". His property in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md), was also raided by FBI. The operation was a part of a criminal investigation started when [Joe Biden](145422.md) was the president.[^cite_note-246] [Donald Trump](4848272.md) said that he had no prior knowledge of the raid.[^cite_note-247] Bolton was neither arrested nor charged with any crime; video footage showed him talking with the agents at his residence.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249]

Reportedly, the raid was conducted after a federal [magistrate judge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magistrate_judge) signed a [search warrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Search_warrant) shortly after another judge in Maryland signed a different search warrant.[^cite_note-250] [JD Vance](53396477.md) said that the raid was not politically motivated and that it was purely "driven by the law". Moments after the raid, FBI director [Kash Patel](62202727.md) issued a statement that "no one is above the law". Attorney General [Pam Bondi](4550623.md) added, "America's safety isn't negotiable. Justice will be pursued. Always".[^cite_note-251] [AP News](18935732.md) reported that Bolton was not present in his home during the raid and that his spokesperson did not give any comments on the situation.[^cite_note-252] Kash Patel said he ordered the raid directly.[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-254]

An [AOL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AOL) email account that Bolton used to submit his draft manuscript was reportedly hacked by a foreign entity.[^cite_note-255] According to CNN, intelligence related to the hack had been collected years prior by the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA), and was used as the basis for the warrant to search Bolton’s house.[^cite_note-256] Categories of potentially classified records that the FBI reported finding at Bolton’s office included travel memo documents with a "secret" label, confidential documents from the [United States Mission to the United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Mission_to_the_United_Nations), confidential documents related to strategic communications, and classified documents related to [weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weapon_of_mass_destruction).[^cite_note-257] By mid-October, prosecutors were preparing an indictment related to his handling of the documents.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259]

### Indictment

On October 16, 2025, Bolton was indicted by a [federal grand jury](34323893.md) in the [District of Maryland](915452.md) on 18 charges related to the mishandling of classified documents. The indictment includes eight counts of unlawful transmission of national defense information and ten counts of unlawful retention of national defense information.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] Many of Trump's critics have seen Bolton's indictment as part of a larger crackdown against other Trump critics, with Bolton being the third Trump critic after [James Comey](2156690.md) and [Letitia James](1598829.md) to be indicted.[^cite_note-arrestandarraignment-262][^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] However, the New York Times has reported that, unlike with other 2025 prosecutions of high-profile Trump critics, the Bolton prosecution process "followed normal department channels, without firings, strong-arm orders from the White House, or forced transfers."[^cite_note-nytimes.com-244]

### Arrest, arraignment and potential plea deal

On October 17, 2025, Bolton surrendered to authorities at a courthouse in [Greenbelt, Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenbelt,_Maryland).[^cite_note-arrestandarraignment-262][^cite_note-indictment-266] While appearing before US magistrate judge Timothy Sullivan, Bolton pled not guilty to the 18-count indictment brought against him.[^cite_note-arrestandarraignment-262][^cite_note-indictment-266]

On June 4, 2026, *[National Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Review)* reported that Bolton had agreed to plead guilty to mishandling classified information in connection with his memoir about his tenure as national security adviser in the first Trump administration. It is unknown if the deal would involve prison time as there is no minimum time for a felony count of illegal retention of classified information. The maximum sentence is five years' imprisonment. The plea deal may end up in a fine as high as \$2.25 million, as Bolton profited from the sale of his memoir.[^cite_note-267] On June 26, 2026, Bolton pled guilty.[^cite_note-268]

## Political positions



Bolton, President Trump and [Kim Jong-un](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Jong-un) in Singapore on June 12, 2018



Bolton with Ukrainian President [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko) and [NATO](21133.md) Secretary General [Jens Stoltenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Stoltenberg) in September 2018



Bolton speaking at the 2017 Conservative Political Action Conference ([CPAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Political_Action_Conference)) in [National Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Harbor,_Maryland), [Maryland](18858.md), on February 24, 2017

He declared himself in an interview with Edward Luce of the *[Financial Times](136566.md)* in 2007 to be a "[Goldwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Goldwater) conservative", as opposed to being a [neoconservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoconservative).[^cite_note-269]

### Unilateralism and Americanism

[Americanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Americanism_\(ideology\)) is Bolton's core belief, according to *The New York Times*:

> Long before Mr. Trump popularized his "America First" slogan, Mr. Bolton termed himself an "Americanist" who prioritized a cold-eyed view of national interests and sovereignty over what they both saw as a starry-eyed fixation on democracy promotion and human rights. They shared a deep skepticism of globalism and multilateralism, a commonality that empowered Mr. Bolton to use his time in the White House to orchestrate the withdrawal of the United States from arms control treaties and other international agreements.[^cite_note-270]

Bolton is skeptical of international organizations and international law, believing them to endanger American [sovereignty](28791.md), and does not believe they have legitimate authority under the U.S. Constitution.[^cite_note-AntiMcMaster-271][^cite_note-272] He criticized the Obama administration's foreign policy for what he perceived as surrendering U.S. sovereignty.[^cite_note-273] He also prefers [unilateralism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unilateralism) over [multilateralism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multilateralism).[^cite_note-AntiMcMaster-271] In a 2000 article in the *[Chicago Journal of International Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Journal_of_International_Law)*, Bolton described himself as a "convinced Americanist", favoring it over what he described as "[globalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalism)".[^cite_note-274] In his roles in the U.S. government, however, Bolton has been more pragmatic in his actions toward international organizations,[^cite_note-275] though according to *[Foreign Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Policy)*, he effectively advanced his views on this subject during his tenure in the Trump Administration.[^cite_note-LivingTheDream-182]

Bolton has criticized the [International Criminal Court](14880.md), seeing it as a threat to U.S. sovereignty.[^cite_note-276] Bolton said: "If the court comes after us, Israel or other US allies, we will not sit quietly. We will ban its judges and prosecutors from entering the United States. We will sanction their funds in the US financial system, and we will prosecute them in the US criminal system."[^cite_note-277]

#### Views on the United Nations



Ambassador Bolton briefing on "The [Human Rights Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Commission) and UN Management Reform" at the New York Foreign Press Center

Bolton has been a strong critic of the United Nations for much of his career.[^cite_note-278] Bolton's opposition to the UN was rooted in a disdain for international organizations, which he believed infringed on the sovereignty of the United States.[^cite_note-\:8-279][^cite_note-\:9-280] He also opposed the [International Criminal Court](14880.md).[^cite_note-\:8-279][^cite_note-\:9-280] In 1994, he stated, "There is no United Nations. There is an international community that occasionally can be led by the only real power left in the world, and that's the United States, when it suits our interests and when we can get others to go along."[^cite_note-281]

He also stated that "The [Secretariat Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Secretariat_Building) in New York has 38 stories. If you lost ten stories today, it wouldn't make a bit of difference."[^cite_note-282] When pressed on the statement during the confirmation process, he responded, "There's not a bureaucracy in the world that couldn't be made leaner."[^cite_note-283] However, in a paper on U.S. participation in the UN, Bolton stated "the United Nations can be a useful instrument in the conduct of American foreign policy."[^cite_note-284] On other occasions, Bolton has made remarks critical of the UN, such as that "If I were redoing the Security Council today, I'd have one permanent member because that's the real reflection of the distribution of power in the world", that member being the United States.[^cite_note-285]

### European Union

Bolton is [a critic](10037.md) of the [European Union](9317.md). In his book *Surrender Is Not an Option*, he criticized the EU for pursuing "the endless process of diplomatic mastication" rather than satisfactorily solving problems, and he labeled the organization's diplomats as "EUroids".[^cite_note-286] He has also criticized the EU for advancing what he considers [liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalism_in_the_United_States) policies.[^cite_note-287] Bolton campaigned in [Ireland](147575.md) against further EU integration in 2008, and he criticized the [Treaty of Lisbon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Lisbon) for expanding EU powers.[^cite_note-288] In 2016, Bolton praised the [UK's referendum vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_Kingdom_European_Union_membership_referendum) to leave the EU,[^cite_note-289] and *[Axios](52436164.md)* reported in January 2019 that Bolton continued to advocate for a [hard Brexit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_Brexit) as National Security Advisor.[^cite_note-290] In a March 2019 interview with [Sky News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_News), Bolton criticized the UK "political class" for not implementing the Brexit vote.[^cite_note-291]

In February 2025, Bolton criticised Trump administration officials for publicly attacking European allies while avoiding similar public pressure on [Russia](25391.md) or [China](5405.md), warning that it could have long-term consequences.[^cite_note-292] During the 2026 Iran war, Bolton criticised European governments for not joining the U.S.-Israeli war effort, arguing that Iran posed a nuclear threat to Europe and that reluctance could encourage Trump to weaken U.S. support for [Ukraine](31750.md).[^cite_note-293]



Bolton, President Trump and German Chancellor [Angela Merkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angela_Merkel) at the G20 Summit in Buenos Aires, December 1, 2018

### Targeted killings of terrorist leaders

In September 2011, when the Obama administration declared the death of [Al Qaeda](1921.md) target and American-born radical [Anwar al-Awlaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_al-Awlaki) in [Yemen](350939.md), Bolton commented "I think it's important as individual Al Qaeda figures and other terrorists are killed that we not read more into it than there is. Consider this analogy if you were around in the 1920s and somebody said, my God, [Vladimir Lenin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Lenin) is dead. The [Bolsheviks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolsheviks) will never recover from this...So while Al-Awlaki's death is significant, I would not read cosmic consequences into it."[^cite_note-John_Bolton-Profile-Right_Web-Institute_for_Policy_Studies-294]

### Whistleblowers

Bolton has called for the execution of whistleblowers such as [Edward Snowden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Snowden) and [Chelsea Manning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chelsea_Manning). Bolton accused Snowden of treason, stating that "Snowden committed treason, he ought to be convicted of that, and then he ought to swing from a tall oak tree".[^cite_note-Newsweek-295] He has stated that he hopes [Julian Assange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_Assange) gets "at least 176 years in jail",[^cite_note-296] and that Manning should be "punished to the fullest extent possible", when asked to elaborate he said that he meant "death".[^cite_note-297]

### Libya

Bolton opposed the [deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disarmament_of_Libya) that George W. Bush made with then Libyan leader [Muammar Gaddafi](53029.md) to eliminate the country's [weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libya_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction) program.[^cite_note-\:1-23] He was in a key role during initial negotiations but his role became limited over time. According to a 2005 study, Bolton was intentionally kept out of the loop so a final agreement could be reached: "Bolton reportedly was unaware of the December 19 WMD agreement until very shortly before its public announcement. And after initially being given a lead role in implementing it, he pushed so hard to backtrack from the agreement that the British convinced the Bush administration to restrict his involvement in the Libya matter."[^cite_note-\:1-23]

Bolton supported the NATO-led [military intervention in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_military_intervention_in_Libya) that toppled the regime of [Muammar Gaddafi](53029.md).[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299]

### Iraq

Bolton is regarded to be an "architect" of the [Iraq War](5043324.md).[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302] In 1998, he was a signatory to a letter sent to President Bill Clinton urging him to remove [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein) from power using U.S. diplomatic, political and military power.[^cite_note-303] He supported the U.S.-led [invasion of Iraq](201936.md) that toppled the regime of Saddam Hussein and continued to stand by his support of the invasion by 2018.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305] In 2007, Bolton said the only mistake the United States made with regard to Iraq was to not leave earlier after the overthrow of Saddam Hussein and tell the Iraqis "Here's a copy of [the Federalist Papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Federalist_Papers). Good luck."[^cite_note-306]

In March 2023, Bolton defended the decision to invade Iraq, claiming that there were concerns that Saddam Hussein would pursue [nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction) and [provide them to terrorist groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein_and_al-Qaeda_link_allegations).[^cite_note-307]

### Kosovo

During 2008, Bolton opposed the [declaration of independence by Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence).[^cite_note-Rudic-308] In 2018 he said, "US policy is that if the two parties can work it out between themselves and reach agreement, we don't exclude territorial adjustments," and that the US or Europe would not "stand in the way if the two parties to the dispute reached a mutually satisfactory settlement."[^cite_note-Rudic-308]

### Western Sahara

In July 2025, Bolton suggested that Algeria offer Donald Trump a gift so that he would abandon American recognition of [Moroccan sovereignty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moroccan-occupied_Western_Sahara) over [annexed Western Sahara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Western_Sahara).[^cite_note-309]

### Israel



Bolton greeting Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) in August 2018



Bolton with Israeli President [Reuven Rivlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuven_Rivlin) in August 2018



President Trump, joined by Bolton and [Netanyahu](56472.md) behind, signs the proclamation [recognizing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_recognition_of_the_Golan_Heights_as_part_of_Israel) Israel's 1981 [annexation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Heights_Law) of the [Golan Heights](7515865.md), March 25, 2019.

Bolton is known for his strong support for [Israel](9282173.md).[^cite_note-al-jazeera-310][^cite_note-311] Bolton opposes the [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution) of creating an independent [Palestinian state](241405.md) alongside the existing state of Israel.[^cite_note-israel-312] Bolton supported moving the U.S. embassy in Israel to Jerusalem in accordance with the [Jerusalem Embassy Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Embassy_Act),[^cite_note-313] and he testified in front of Congress in 2017 on the matter.[^cite_note-314] In 2010, Bolton co-founded the [Friends of Israel Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friends_of_Israel_Initiative) with 12 other international figures.[^cite_note-jpost-20100531-315]

Bolton supports what he calls the "Three State Solution" in order to determine the political status of Gaza and the West Bank. Under Bolton's Three State Solution, Israel would [annex settlements](60995835.md) it has built in the West Bank since the conclusion of the Six-Days War in 1967, Egypt would annex and administer the Gaza Strip, and Jordan would annex and administer the remaining portion of the West Bank not annexed by Israel. Bolton's Three State proposal would not result in the creation of an independent Palestinian state.[^cite_note-316]

Bolton opposed the [2023 Gaza war ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire).[^cite_note-317] In December 2023, he proposed [resettling Palestinians from Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_Israeli_resettlement_of_the_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-318] In April 2024, Bolton said that the [Gaza war](74998519.md) "is an Iranian war against Israel, fought through terrorist proxies", and the only way to end the [suffering of the people of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_humanitarian_crisis_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)) "is to eliminate Hamas, which is the cause of the suffering."[^cite_note-319]

### North Korea and Iran

Bolton has advocated for pre-emptive strikes against [North Korea](21255.md) and [Iran](14653.md). In March 2018, he suggested that [South Korea](27019.md) take [North Korea](21255.md) and terminate the North Korean regime as the only "diplomatic option", and said that the war between the two countries is their problem and not the United States' problem.[^cite_note-\:14-320]

In 2006, Bolton attempted to bring prosecution against Iranian president [Mahmoud Ahmadinejad](7907317.md) for [incitement to genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incitement_to_genocide) in the [International Court of Justice](14918.md), along with [Alan Dershowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Dershowitz), [Dore Gold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dore_Gold), and other experts from the United States, Canada, and Israel, based on Ahmadinejad's comments that "[Israel must be wiped off the map](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_must_be_wiped_off_the_map)".[^cite_note-321]

In 2008, Bolton said: "The idea here is not to have much larger hostilities, but to stop the Iranians from engaging in the hostilities that they're already doing against us inside Iraq. And they're doing much the same by aiding the Taliban in Afghanistan. So this is not provocative or preemptive, this is entirely responsive on our part."[^cite_note-\:14-320] In 2018, Bolton stated: "Russia, China, Syria, Iran, North Korea. These are regimes that make agreements and lie about them. A national security policy that is based on the faith that regimes like that will honor their commitments is doomed to failure."[^cite_note-weekly-322] He also said, "Our goal should be [regime change](154074.md) in Iran."[^cite_note-israel-312] *[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker)* described the people who have worked with Bolton as saying "he is focused less on North Korea than on Iran". H. R. McMaster has reportedly told Dexter Fikins that Bolton has had "\[the] [anal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anal_retentiveness) focus on Iran for twenty years".[^cite_note-FiDe-323]

Unlike several of President Trump's early national security officials like National Security Adviser [H.R. McMaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H.R._McMaster) and Secretary of State [Rex Tillerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_Tillerson), Bolton campaigned to press President Trump for a complete withdrawal from the 2015 nuclear deal with Iran (the JCPOA) and rejected the idea it could be fixed. Unable at the time to directly present his position to President Trump, Bolton published his proposal on how to withdraw from the Iran deal in an August 28, 2017 [National Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Review) Online article.[^cite_note-Iran1-324] After he was named to succeed McMaster as Nation Security Adviser in April 2018, Bolton pressed the President [to withdraw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_withdrawal_from_the_Joint_Comprehensive_Plan_of_Action) from the [Iran nuclear deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_nuclear_deal), a decision President Trump announced a month later.[^cite_note-Iran2-170] Bolton has been one of the biggest anti-Iran hawks in the Trump administration.[^cite_note-325]

Speaking to a meeting of Iranian exile group [Mujahedin-e-Khalq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Mujahedin_of_Iran) in March 2018, Bolton said the Trump administration should follow the goal of regime change in Iran and that "before 2019, we here will celebrate in Tehran!"[^cite_note-326]

In 2019, Bolton, [Israeli](9282173.md) Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and Secretary of State [Mike Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pompeo) successfully sabotaged Trump's attempts to open diplomatic channels with Iran.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328]

On January 3, 2020, the high-level Iranian General, [Qasem Soleimani](16805217.md), was [killed in a U.S. drone strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Baghdad_International_Airport_airstrike), which considerably heightened the [existing tensions](310477.md) between the two countries. In a tweet, Bolton called the airstrike a "long in the making, this was a decisive blow against Iran's malign [Quds Force](4028355.md) activities worldwide. Hope this is the first step to regime change in Tehran."[^cite_note-329]

In early 2026, Bolton actively defended joint [U.S.-Israeli military strikes against Iran](82533582.md), describing them as "justifiable and necessary" to achieve regime change. He argued that destroying the Iranian regime was the only way to eliminate its [nuclear threat](1757165.md).[^cite_note-330]

In January and February 2026, Bolton continued to argue that an [Iranian regime](50913538.md) change could be pursued without a large [ground invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion), instead by striking instruments of Iranian state power, including the [IRGC](1285472.md) and [Basij militia](2892082.md).[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332] In March and April 2026, he criticised Trump's [Iran-war](82533582.md) planning and ceasefire approach, opposing an extended ceasefire and supporting militarily reopening the strait rather than making concessions to Tehran.[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334] In June 2026, Bolton criticised a reported U.S.-Iran framework deal, saying Iran had outmanoeuvred Trump and that the United States had sacrificed strategic leverage to reopen the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md) and lower energy prices.[^cite_note-335]

#### People's Mujahedin of Iran

Prior to it being de-listed by the U.S. as a [Foreign Terrorist Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_State_Department_list_of_Foreign_Terrorist_Organizations) in 2012, Bolton spoke in favor of the [People's Mujahedin of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Mujahedin_of_Iran) (also known as the Mujahedin-e Khalq, or MEK),[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] MEK has opposed the Iranian [theocratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theocratic) state since shortly after the 1979 [Iranian Revolution](347268.md).[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-StateList-339] According to his financial disclosure, he was given \$40,000 for his 2016 speech to MEK.[^cite_note-FiDe-323] According to the [5 U.S.C. app. § 101](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethics_in_Government_Act)-required 'US Public Financial Disclosure Report' (2018) for Bolton, released by [Al-Monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Monitor), he has received \$40,000 of speaking fee for "Global Events–European Iranian Events" on June 1, 2017,[^cite_note-340] the same day he made a speech for the MEK in a gathering in Paris, France.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344]

### Russia



Bolton with Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) in June 2018

In 2013, after NSA whistleblower [Edward Snowden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Snowden) had been granted asylum in [Russia](25391.md), Bolton said: "I think in order to focus Putin's thinking, we need to do things that cause him pain as well. And while I know that not having a chance to have a bilateral meeting with his buddy [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) will cause Putin to lose sleep, it's not damaging Russian interests."[^cite_note-weekly-322] Bolton accused Snowden of treason, stating that "Snowden committed treason, he ought to be convicted of that, and then he ought to swing from a tall oak tree".[^cite_note-Newsweek-295]

Russian Senator [Aleksey Pushkov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksey_Pushkov), former chairman of the [State Duma](1010187.md) Committee on International Affairs, said after Bolton's appointment: "Bolton, along with Bush, Cheney and Rumsfeld, was an ardent supporter of the war in Iraq. A supporter of jihadists for the sake of overthrowing \[Syrian President Bashar al-Assad]. A great specialist in interventions and aggression, and adept at the use of force. McMaster is a general. Bolton is the ideologue of a [new cold war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_cold_war), a convinced opponent of Russia."[^cite_note-345]



Bolton meets with Russian Defense Minister [Sergey Shoygu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergey_Shoygu) in Moscow in October 2018.

In a June 2017 article entitled "Vladimir Putin looked Trump in the eye and lied to him. We negotiate with Russia at our peril," Bolton called [Russian interference in the 2016 United States elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_interference_in_the_2016_United_States_elections) "a true act of war."[^cite_note-346] As Trump's national security advisor in July 2018, Bolton referred to the investigation into the Russian interference as "the Russia witch hunt".[^cite_note-347]

In a December 2021 article entitled "Now Is the Time for NATO to Stand Up to Russia," Bolton called for an aggressive response to [Moscow's troops build-up along the Ukraine border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prelude_to_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), before the [full-scale invasion](79400268.md) commenced in February 2022.[^cite_note-348]

On July 6, 2023, U.S. President [Joe Biden](145422.md) approved the provision of [cluster munitions](79745.md) to Ukraine.[^cite_note-349] Bolton hailed the decision as "an excellent idea".[^cite_note-350]

In February 2025, Bolton opposed a hasty Ukraine settlement and warned that a peacekeeping force freezing the front lines could amount to a de facto partition of Ukraine.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352] In November 2025, Bolton criticised a reported US-Russia Ukraine peace plan and said Trump did not care about Ukraine.[^cite_note-353]

### China



Bolton, President Trump and [Chinese leader](262766.md) [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) at the G20 Summit in Osaka, June 28, 2019

In 2018, Bolton criticized Washington's [One-China policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-China_policy), under which [Taiwan](25734.md), officially known as the Republic of China, is not recognized as an independent nation, with recognition being given only to the [People's Republic of China](5405.md).[^cite_note-al-jazeera-310]

On China's notoriety in [intellectual property](14724.md) matters, he remarked in 2018 that "There's simply no excuse for the stealing of intellectual property, forced technology transfers it's sometimes called."[^cite_note-al-jazeera-310]

Bolton said in October 2018 that the United States' need to counter a [Chinese arms buildup in the Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernization_of_the_People's_Liberation_Army), including within [South China Sea](32005536.md#United_States), was one of the reasons for their move to withdraw from the [Intermediate-Range Nuclear Forces Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intermediate-Range_Nuclear_Forces_Treaty) with Russia, because China is not a signatory to the treaty.[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355]

On January 20, 2021, the Chinese government imposed [sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_sanctions) against Bolton and 27 other Trump administration officials that "planned, promoted and executed a series of crazy moves, gravely interfered in China's internal affairs, undermined China's interests, offended the Chinese people, and seriously disrupted China-U.S. relations". The sanctions ban them from entering China, including Hong Kong and Macau, and restrict companies and institutions associated with them from doing business in China.[^cite_note-356]

### India

In September 2025, Bolton criticized Trump's 50% [tariff](78448911.md) imposed on India over [India's trade with Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_and_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and questioned why similar tariff measures weren’t applied to China, Turkey, or Pakistan. He also criticized Trump's claim about his role in peace between India and Pakistan after the [2025 Pahalgam Attack](79778862.md) and the subsequent [2025 India-Pakistan conflict](79896622.md).[^cite_note-357]

### Latin America



Bolton with Brazil's president-elect [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md) in Rio de Janeiro in November 2018

In a speech as National Security Advisor on November 1, 2018, Bolton praised [Brazil](3383.md)'s president-elect [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md) and [Colombia](5222.md)'s president [Iván Duque Márquez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Duque_M%C3%A1rquez), both right-wing conservatives, calling them "like-minded" partners. In the speech, he also framed Bolsonaro's [election victory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Brazilian_general_election) as a "positive sign" for Latin America, and he criticized [Cuba](5042481.md), [Venezuela](32374.md), and [Nicaragua](21362.md) as a "[troika of tyranny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troika_of_tyranny)."[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361]

### Criticism of the Obama administration

In December 2012, Bolton suggested that Secretary of State [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton) had faked a concussion to avoid testifying before Congress regarding the attack on the U.S. Consulate in Benghazi, Libya. Bolton stated "When you don't want to go to a meeting or conference or an event you have a '[diplomatic illness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_illness)'. And this is a diplomatic illness to beat the band."[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364]

In 2010, he wrote a foreword for the book *The Post-American Presidency: The Obama Administration's War on America*, authored by far-right anti-Muslim commentators [Pamela Geller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamela_Geller) and [Robert Spencer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Spencer_\(author\)).[^cite_note-thinkprogress.org-365][^cite_note-366] Bolton endorsed their book, writing: "This book carries forward the ongoing and increasingly widespread critique of Barack Obama as our first post-American president. What it recounts is disturbing, and its broader implications are more disturbing still."[^cite_note-367]

## Alleged assassination plot

On August 10, 2022, an Iranian national and member of Iran's [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](1285472.md), Shahram Poursafi, was charged by the [United States Department of Justice](52563.md) in an October 2021 [plot to allegedly murder Bolton](80345339.md), likely in retaliation for the January 2020 death of Qasem Soleimani.[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369] In response to the plot, Bolton was granted a protective detail from the [United States Secret Service](58221.md) on the orders of President [Joe Biden](145422.md).[^cite_note-370] This protection was later rescinded by President Trump upon his return to office on January 20, 2025.[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373]

## Personal life

Bolton married Christina Bolton in 1972; they divorced in 1983.[^cite_note-Heavy20190910-374] He has been married to Gretchen Smith Bolton, a financial planner with [AXA Advisors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AXA), since January 1986.[^cite_note-Heavy20190910-374] She was born in [Kansas City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City,_Missouri) in 1945 and had been married once before, divorcing in 1973. Together they have a daughter, Jennifer, and have resided in [Bethesda, Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bethesda,_Maryland) since 1986.[^cite_note-bustlewhoisgetchensmithbolton-375][^cite_note-376] Bolton is a [Lutheran](23371382.md).[^cite_note-377]
